{"forms":[{"form":"the divergence theorem","tags":["canonical"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"name","senses":[{"glosses":["A three-dimensional generalization of the fundamental theorem of calculus, which states that given a solid region W bounded by a closed surface S oriented by a unit normal vector mathbf n, if a vector field mathbf F whose component functions have continuous first partial derivatives in W, then the flux of mathbf F through S is equal to the triple integral of the divergence of mathbf F over W, or ∬_S mathbf F· mathbf n,dS=∭_W∇· mathbf F,dV."],"topics":["calculus","mathematics","sciences"]}],"upstream_line":10257456,"upstream_offset":24718931207,"upstream_record_sha256":"df3d13f7917a6753278396e0b580892f771d8d390de09a82e522b5634ea7c4d0","word":"divergence theorem"}

{"forms":[{"form":"parliamentary trains","tags":["plural"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"glosses":["A cheap, slow public passenger train as provided for under the Railway Regulation Act 1844 (which ordered railways to run at least one train a day each way, at a cost to passengers of no more than one penny a mile, on every railway line in the country)."],"tags":["UK","historical"],"topics":["rail-transport","railways","transport"]},{"glosses":["A passenger train that serves a line or station very infrequently (often only once a day or once a week), allowing the train operator to effectively end the service without getting official permission to close the line."],"synonyms":[{"word":"parliamentary service"},{"word":"parliamentary"}],"tags":["UK"],"topics":["rail-transport","railways","transport"]}],"upstream_line":7729385,"upstream_offset":17696647721,"upstream_record_sha256":"1deb1680fe57e3321f0d3e1481e6117f1190176e84b91635f345e4def3fd9edd","word":"parliamentary train"}
